## #119 Choco Screech-Owl — *Megascops centralis* — v4 VERIFIED

- [Original concept](https://github.com/janeytlin-eng/game-owl-ket/blob/d206093f1bdc0044ec854b0740ac0b41b8efb744/owl-pet/design/concepts/119-megascops-centralis-v4.png?raw=1) · [fixed archive](https://github.com/janeytlin-eng/game-owl-ket/commit/d206093f1bdc0044ec854b0740ac0b41b8efb744) · [research](../research/species/119-megascops-centralis-v4.md) · [run](../production/runs/20261031T143516+0800-cycle-5.md)
- VERIFIED; exact five image tuples and complete manifests 609/608/599 read back; `PASS_WITH_LIMITATIONS`; science false; unique175/251; batch5/6 next6.
- V4 keeps the primary-study-supported formed barred remiges but shortens the visible folded-wing silhouette above the feet/sill, embeds it in gray mesoptile, hides the tail and retains bilateral cavity support. Adults remain near-equal because sex-size difference is unknown.

### Separate “formed” from “adult-proportioned”

An advanced owl juvenile may have pennaceous primaries and secondaries while still having no measurable tail. Do not erase documented flight feathers merely because the existing drawing looks mature; instead compare silhouette length, layering regularity, tail projection and support against the exact stage evidence. Applied and crop-checked on #119 v4; not a universal chronology rule.

### Repair the archive source before repairing plumage

If the latest binary conflicts with its immutable run size/hash and cannot be opened, do not treat a valid PNG header as a usable image. Preserve the bad path, select the latest fully decoded predecessor, record the recovery boundary, and create a new version. After generation, crop review must distinguish a shorter silhouette from persistent repeated feather outlines; #117 v9 required a second juvenile-only repaint. Applied and visually checked here; not a universal feather rule.

When an institutional field caption shows “shortly before leaving” and later branch exploration but no hatch date, use it to bound feather maturity and support/action. Do not transfer a precise age from another population. Applied and visually rechecked on #59 v4; not yet a universal rule.

The just-out, still-flightless brancher is charcoal-gray above, pale below, short-winged/short-tailed and fully bark-supported by both talons; adult sexes share plumage and the female is only subtly fuller. Exact age/locality/subspecies/feather sequence/standing ratio/first-flight day remain unknown; science false.
- Archive: manifest535 VERIFIED at fixed commit `aeee17b44f7b57994e2580e3761ec3d32d905104`; non-truncated 1813-entry tree, exact five image blobs/sizes and source-index/research/run markers passed.

### A climbing brancher needs stage-correct feathering as well as support

A bark-supported pose can still be developmentally wrong when image generation adds a mature fan of layered remiges and regular pale rows. For a just-out nonflying Great Gray Owl, inspect a tight crop and require the wing to remain compact, rounded and down-softened while talons, bill/wing assistance and body-to-tree contact remain plausible. Applied and visually rechecked once on #08 v5; species/stage bounded.

Exact age/iris/sex, flight threshold and quantified dimorphism remain unknown. Fixed-commit readback passed at `1e4a9aacdc032cc5a301d3f6c7516f93050cc947`; four exact JPEG/crop path/blob/size checks passed. Science false.

### Crop review must decide whether a “shortened” wing is still adult-like

A full-frame edit can visually shorten a juvenile wing while retaining a readable fan of long layered remiges and pale spot rows. Reopen a tight crop: if the fan remains legible, remove it and require the wing boundary to dissolve into down-dominant short coverts. Applied twice and visually rechecked on #101 v6; this is a stage-specific correction, not a universal owl rule.

### Late pre-fledgling flight readiness requires developed wings

Do not reuse the short-wing correction from younger owl stages when exact-taxon evidence documents climbing, flapping and first flight from about 46 days. Preserve fresh/soft feather quality and residual down instead of shortening the remiges.

## #90 American Barn Owl — *Tyto furcata pratincola* — v4 science revision

- Target: [90-tyto-furcata-v4.jpg](concepts/90-tyto-furcata-v4.jpg)
- Research: [species card](../knowledge/species/90-tyto-furcata.md) · [source index](../research/SOURCE_INDEX.md#2026-10-29--90-tyto-furcata-pratincola-v4--documented-p20-standing-down-dominant-stage)
- Stage: VERIFIED at `0932faa36441386c748694150eadd2f1af54a998`; six exact image path/blob/size readbacks passed. Review: PASS_WITH_LIMITATIONS after one juvenile-only wing edit. Exact-taxon P20 evidence supports standing/walking while still white-down-dominant, not branch perching or flight. Science false.

### Locomotor readiness does not imply mature remiges

- Discovery: the first render converted P20 standing ability into long, layered adult-like folded remiges.
- Evidence: Wagner et al. 2022 Figure 1 and observations show P20 standing/walking with dominant white down and only emerging dark wing feathers; flight starts near P60 in that hand-raised series.
- Applied here: shorten and soften only the juvenile wing, keep emerging dark quills close to the body, no developed tail, preserve full floor support.
- Verified here: edited full 1536×1024 plate and 560×650 juvenile crop reopened. This validation is limited to the selected P20 boundary and does not establish a universal wild timetable.

## #89 Indian Eagle-Owl — *Bubo bengalensis* — v3 science revision

- Target: [89-bubo-bengalensis-v3.jpg](concepts/89-bubo-bengalensis-v3.jpg)
- Research: [species card](../knowledge/species/89-bubo-bengalensis.md) · [source index](../research/SOURCE_INDEX.md#2026-10-29--89-bubo-bengalensis-v3--c35-day-full-juvenile-plumage-pre-flight-inference-boundary)
- Current review: PASS_WITH_LIMITATIONS after one juvenile-only edit. V3 selects the separately documented c.35-day full-juvenile-plumage stage, but does not infer flight. Adults remain near-equal height; “female heavier” is not asserted. Exact age, feather-tract colour, flight threshold and local morph remain unknown; science false.

### Full juvenile plumage is not adult-regular pattern or flight proof

- Discovery: the first render converted “full juvenile plumage” into regular bright white wing spots and hard adult-like layered remiges.
- Evidence: BEN-RAM2009 supplies the c.35-day plumage boundary; BEN-PANDE-DAHANUKAR2011 reports loose coupling among wing, tail, body mass and other growth characters.
- Applied here: a juvenile-only edit softened/shortened the folded wing, made pale flecks irregular and retained residual down plus a short/hidden tail.
- Verified here: full plate and 560×650 crop reopened; supported crouch and age contrast passed. This process lesson is validated only for #89 v3’s selected evidence boundary.

- Stage: VERIFIED at `daf637f05014949846ea1d3c60a53117cc63c381`; recursive tree and exact blob/size pairs for concept, generated/reviewed JPEG+PNG and crop passed. V3 selects a 7–10-day white-down, brown-eyed cavity chick with just-opening short remige sheaths and no rectrix fan. One edit removed long patterned juvenile feathers; full and crop recheck passed. Science false.

### “Pin feathers beginning” forbids long patterned vane fans

- Discovery: imagegen turned a few opening remige sheaths into long, white-patterned vaned feathers despite the early-age prompt.
- Applied here: require short grey sheaths hugging the wing stub, minimal soft tips, no patterned vane fan and no rectrices.
- Verified here: edited full plate and juvenile crop passed while adults/text/support stayed stable. This is validated for #80’s selected stage only.

## #79 Collared Scops Owl — *Otus lettia* — v3 science revision

- Concept: [79-otus-lettia-v3.jpg](concepts/79-otus-lettia-v3.jpg)
- Research: [species card](../knowledge/species/79-otus-lettia.md) · [source index](../research/SOURCE_INDEX.md#2026-10-29--79-otus-lettia-v3--1115-day-cavity-nestling-feather-boundary)
- Stage: VERIFIED at `731714d9ca44a9e750f413be73b28ce57676222a`; recursive tree and exact blob/size pairs for concept, generated/reviewed JPEG+PNG and crop passed. V3 uses an 11–15-day cavity nestling from a species-specific Thai chronology, distinct from v1–v2’s older perched juvenile. Open eyes, pale down, beginning bill bristles, short pin-remiges, no rectrices and belly/sternum support passed full and crop review. Science false.

### Use chronological feather boundaries before generic “fledgling” texture

- Discovery: the species-specific sequence separates early remige/semiplume growth (6–10 d), bill-bristle onset (11–15 d), rectrix onset (16–20 d) and fully feathered/fledged state (21–25 d).
- Applied here: choose 11–15 d and explicitly exclude rectrices, standing, climbing and flight; retain full cavity-floor support.
- Verified here: the first full plate and juvenile crop passed without edit. This validation is limited to #79 and the Thai study’s range, not a universal scops-owl timetable.

## #78 Marsh Owl — *Asio capensis* — v3 science revision

- Concept: [78-asio-capensis-v3.jpg](concepts/78-asio-capensis-v3.jpg)
- Research: [source index](../research/SOURCE_INDEX.md#2026-10-29--78-asio-capensis-v3--early-fledgling-capability-and-current-taxonomy-boundary)
- Stage: VERIFIED at `a8259c009bdbea08655e0a91f819d2810e7ee5fb`; recursive tree and exact blob/size pairs for concept, generated/reviewed JPEG+PNG and crop passed. V3 uses an evidence-bounded 29–35-day early fledgling and preserves v2's 14–18-day crawler as history. One juvenile-only edit removed adult-regular wing rows/hard remiges and shortened the tail. Exact age/tract chronology/first-flight threshold/sex ratio remain unknown; science false.

### Separate locomotor readiness from adult-like vane patterning

- Discovery: an early fledgling may stand and step with developed folded wings, but the first generation converted that capability into crisp adult-regular wing rows, hard layered remiges and a mature tail.
- Applied here: retain supported stepping and wing development while explicitly softening feather edges, making markings irregular and shortening the tail.
- Verified here: the edited full plate and juvenile/feet crop were reopened and passed; adults, labels, habitat and support stayed unchanged. This is evidence for #78's selected stage, not a universal owl rule.

## #50 Mottled Owl — *Strix virgata* — v3 science revision

- Concept: [50-strix-virgata-v3.png](concepts/50-strix-virgata-v3.png)
- Research: [species card](../knowledge/species/50-strix-virgata.md) · [source index](../research/SOURCE_INDEX.md#2026-10-29-50-strix-virgata-v3-quantified-dimorphism-correction)
- Stage: VERIFIED at `defa5d15254d309b8d0959b1512290a2015d689e`; fixed concept/generated/reviewed blob IDs, exact byte sizes and text markers read back. Female body volume follows Tikal measurements without exaggerated height. One juvenile-only edit removed the long layered pale-spotted wing; science false.

### Incremental down-dominant nestling wing rule

For a sourced down-dominant cavity nestling, “shorter wing” is not a sufficient prompt boundary. Explicitly forbid a readable layered remex row and pale edge spots, require any emerging wing boundary to dissolve into natal down, retain a concealed tail and full belly/two-foot support, and re-open both master and derivative. Applied and visually rechecked on #50 v3; it does not establish a universal daily feather sequence.

## 2026-10-26 · #18 Megascops kennicottii v2 evidence-bounded rebuild

![#18 Western Screech-Owl v2](concepts/18-megascops-kennicottii-v2.png)

Rebuilt one adult-male / early-hatchling / adult-female concept sheet. Adults retain yellow eyes, deep dark bills, small ear tufts, compact square-headed gray-brown bark pattern and near-equal displayed height. The center hatchling uses the source-bounded closed-eye white-down condition, with short down-covered wing buds, concealed tail, and full head/belly/two-foot support on sparse natural cavity debris. First render passed original-resolution review without edit. Exact hatchling age, eye-opening day, continuous feather sequence, quantified sex difference outside sampled populations, regional morph and universal cavity geometry remain unknown; scientifically_verified=false. Archive commit `cd2e596fc04013b904d2faaf2b9fdc55026660f4`.

## 2026-10-26 · #17 Megascops asio v4 evidence-bounded rebuild

![#17 Eastern Screech-Owl v4](concepts/17-megascops-asio-v4.png)

Rebuilt one adult-male / early-hatchling / adult-female concept sheet. Adults retain yellow eyes, small ear tufts, gray-green bills and gray-morph bark pattern at near-equal displayed height. The center hatchling uses the source-bounded closed-eye white-down condition, with short down-covered wing buds, concealed tail, and full head/belly/two-foot support on sparse natural cavity debris. First render passed original-resolution review without edit. Exact hatchling age, eye-opening day, continuous feather sequence, regional morph variation and quantified sex difference remain unknown; scientifically_verified=false. Archive commit `bccad4774ccebdd50a97ae0f769896dcdfb4c4a1`.

## 19 · 须角鸮 v1

![须角鸮v1](concepts/19-megascops-trichopsis-v1.png)

[研究档案](../knowledge/species/19-megascops-trichopsis.md)。成年雄鸟、成年雌鸟与接近离巢幼鸟；浅黄橄榄色喙、橙黄色眼、脸部须状羽与粗黑斑点用于区别西美角鸮。已目视检查，待用户确认和科学校准。

## 20 · 裸胫角鸮 v1

![裸胫角鸮v1](concepts/20-megascops-clarkii-v1.png)

[研究档案](../knowledge/species/20-megascops-clarkii.md)。成年雄鸟、成年雌鸟与约三周龄洞巢幼鸟；采用湿润山地云雾林及天然树洞。初稿把裸露腿段夸张得过长，v1归档图已编辑为上段有羽、仅短下段裸露并完成目视复查；待用户确认和科学校准。

## 21 · 热带角鸮 v1

![热带角鸮v1](concepts/21-megascops-choliba-v1.png)

[研究档案](../knowledge/species/21-megascops-choliba.md)。成年雄鸟、成年雌鸟与11–13日龄洞巢幼鸟；采用灰褐型、开阔热带林缘和枯死tucumã棕榈天然洞。首图幼鸟翼羽偏成熟，归档图已回退为白绒羽为主、仅有少量初生羽轴并完成目视复查；待用户确认和科学校准。

## 22 · 波多黎各鸮 v1

![波多黎各鸮v1](concepts/22-gymnasio-nudipes-v1.png)

[研究档案](../knowledge/species/22-gymnasio-nudipes.md)。采用现行学名 *Gymnasio nudipes*，记录旧名 *Megascops nudipes* 与 *Otus nudipes*；成年雄鸟、成年雌鸟和年龄未定的洞巢蓬松幼鸟。首图幼鸟出现成熟层叠翼羽，归档图已回退为短幼羽并复查；待用户确认和科学校准。

## 23 · 美洲角鸮 v1

![美洲角鸮v1](concepts/23-psiloscops-flammeolus-v1.png)

[研究档案](../knowledge/species/23-psiloscops-flammeolus.md)。采用现行学名 *Psiloscops flammeolus*，记录旧名 *Otus flammeolus*；成年雄鸟、成年雌鸟与闭眼白绒羽初生幼鸟。成年以深褐近黑虹膜、短而贴伏耳羽及灰褐—锈色火焰状纹区别相邻角鸮。首图幼鸟头部贴地过低，归档图改为木屑坡承托头颈、轻张喙的低伏姿态并复查；待用户确认和科学校准。

## 24 · 北方鸺鹠 v1

![北方鸺鹠v1](concepts/24-glaucidium-californicum-v1.png)

[研究档案](../knowledge/species/24-glaucidium-californicum.md)。采用现行学名 *Glaucidium californicum*，记录部分旧资料中的 *G. gnoma* 对应；成年雄鸟、成年雌鸟与近期离巢幼鸟。成年以圆头无耳簇、黄眼黄喙、长横斑尾、纵纹下体及后颈假眼斑识别。首图幼鸟尾羽过长且横斑过熟，归档图已缩短尾羽并弱化成年斑纹后复查；待用户确认和科学校准。

## 25 · 仙人掌褐鸺鹠 v1

![仙人掌褐鸺鹠v1](concepts/25-glaucidium-brasilianum-cactorum-v1.png)

[研究档案](../knowledge/species/25-glaucidium-brasilianum-cactorum.md)。本组限定北美西部亚种 *Glaucidium brasilianum cactorum*；成年雄鸟、略丰满的成年雌鸟与近期离巢幼鸟。成年采用无耳簇、黄眼、锈褐无白斑背、长横斑尾和后颈假眼斑方向；幼鸟采用较巧克力褐、冠纹较少、下体更白且较短尾的离巢阶段。已目视检查，待用户确认和分类、比例、幼鸟阶段及假眼斑视角科学校准。

## #153 · Maned Owl — *Jubula lettii* v1

[Original PNG](concepts/153-jubula-lettii-v1.png) · [species record](../knowledge/species/153-jubula-lettii.md) · [cycle record](../production/runs/2026-10-22T2031+0800-cycle-3.md)

Adult male, adult female and one age-unknown older immature. Adults keep near-equal visible scale because sex-specific measurements were not found. The initial juvenile had adult-like long layered remiges and a long barred tail; the archived edit shortens/softens both while retaining white juvenile facial feathers and two-foot branch support. Actual edited PNG re-viewed; concept only, pending user confirmation and scientific calibration.


## #154 · Jamaican Owl — *Pseudoscops grammicus* v1

[Original PNG](concepts/154-pseudoscops-grammicus-v1.png) · [species record](../knowledge/species/154-pseudoscops-grammicus.md) · [cycle record](../production/runs/2026-10-22T2051+0800-cycle-4.md)

Adult male, adult female and age-unknown downy young. Full image viewed: adult diagnostics and three labels present; juvenile has short soft wings and hidden/very short tail with both feet on cavity substrate. No edit required. Pending user confirmation and scientific validation.


## 155 · Rufous Fishing Owl · *Scotopelia ussheri* v1
![155-scotopelia-ussheri-v1](concepts/155-scotopelia-ussheri-v1.png)

Adult male, adult female and evidence-bounded older juvenile in Upper Guinea river forest. Actual image reviewed; complete PNG verified. Exact juvenile age, sexual size ratio and fine feather geometry remain pending scientific calibration.


## #109 · Northern White-faced Owl — *Ptilopsis leucotis* v6
[Original PNG](concepts/109-ptilopsis-leucotis-v6.png) · [species record](../knowledge/species/109-ptilopsis-leucotis.md) · [cycle record](../production/runs/2026-10-23T0431+0800-cycle-6.md)

Revision replaces repeated bright juvenile wing scallops with shorter, softer gray-brown developing wings while retaining formed tips and full branch support. Actual targeted edit passed limited review; age, continuous feather sequence, sex ratio and toe-feather extent remain pending, with no scientific pass transferred.


## #123 · Giant Scops Owl — *Otus gurneyi* v4
[Original PNG](concepts/123-otus-gurneyi-v4.png) · [species record](../knowledge/species/123-otus-gurneyi.md) · [cycle record](../production/runs/2026-10-23T0627+0800-cycle-1.md)

Revision of the older photographed-stage juvenile: an initial resumed render again exposed long folded remiges reaching the feet, so a targeted edit concealed the long wing and tail beneath soft gray-brown juvenile plumage while keeping two-foot support. Adults, brown eyes, black-spotted tufts, labels and habitat remain. Scientific calibration is still pending.


## #125 · Luzon Boobook — *Ninox philippensis* v4
[Original PNG](concepts/125-ninox-philippensis-v4.png) · [species record](../knowledge/species/125-ninox-philippensis.md) · [cycle record](../production/runs/2026-10-23T0832+0800-cycle-2.md)

Revision rebuilds both adults with plain unmarked matte-brown crowns while preserving legitimate white wing spots. The first juvenile render was too mature; the reviewed edit shortens and rounds its formed wing, hides the tail and retains residual buff down plus two-foot branch support. Scientific calibration remains pending.


## #113 · Christmas Island Boobook — *Ninox natalis* v5
[Original PNG](concepts/113-ninox-natalis-v5.png) · [cycle record](../production/runs/2026-10-23T0832+0800-cycle-3.md)

Revision replaces the initial adult-length juvenile wing and long tail with a shorter rounded formed wing, concealed tail, irregular ventral markings and natural two-foot support. Scientific calibration remains pending.


## #128 · Cloud-forest Screech-Owl — *Megascops marshalli* v6
[Original PNG](concepts/128-megascops-marshalli-v6.png) · [species record](../knowledge/species/128-megascops-marshalli.md) · [cycle record](../production/runs/2026-10-23T0929+0800-cycle-4.md)

Revision narrows and lowers the contrast of the middle adult female's hind-crown band, breaking it into individually tipped posterior feathers instead of a bright continuous arc. Actual full plate reviewed; no forehead V, cap or collar, and the other two birds, older-immature formed wings, supports and labels remain. Scientific calibration remains pending.


## #105 · Southern White-faced Owl — *Ptilopsis granti* v5
[Original PNG](concepts/105-ptilopsis-granti-v5.png) · [species record](../knowledge/species/105-ptilopsis-granti.md) · [cycle record](../production/runs/2026-10-23T1031+0800-cycle-5.md)

Revision changes the older juvenile's face from adult-white to gray-brown and reduces bright decorative body/covert edges. Actual plate reviewed: developing wing remains formed but shorter than adults, tail concealed, both feet supported; adults and labels intact. Scientific calibration remains pending.


## #117 · Sulawesi Scops Owl — *Otus manadensis* v6
[Original PNG](concepts/117-otus-manadensis-v6.png) · [species record](../knowledge/species/117-otus-manadensis.md) · [cycle record](../production/runs/2026-10-23T1134+0800-cycle-6.md)

Revision preserves the orphan v5 and follows one rejected draft with a second targeted juvenile-only edit. Actual final plate reviewed: broad softer irregular gray-brown ventral bars replace most bright regular scallop rows while heavier crown/mantle barring, short formed wings, barred tail and branch support remain. Residual fine low-contrast arcs and exact developmental calibration remain pending; scientific verification is false.


## #90 · American Barn Owl — *Tyto furcata* v3
[Original PNG](concepts/90-tyto-furcata-v3.png) · [species record](../knowledge/species/90-tyto-furcata.md) · [cycle record](../production/runs/2026-10-23T1233+0800-cycle-1.md)

Revision corrects the v2 overshoot in sex-size display: the right adult female is now only modestly taller and fuller than the male while retaining darker, more spotted plumage. Actual plate reviewed; juvenile stage, supports, labels and environment remain intact. Exact ratio and scientific calibration remain pending.


## #159 · Sri Lanka Bay Owl — *Phodilus assimilis* v2
[Original PNG](concepts/159-phodilus-assimilis-v2.png) · [species record](../knowledge/species/159-phodilus-assimilis.md) · [cycle record](../production/runs/2026-10-24T0933+0800-cycle-1.md)

Recovery revision preserves the previously orphaned v1 and registers both versions. Actual full-resolution v2 review confirms the juvenile folded wing is shorter, rounder and more down-covered, with no exposed long tail and both feet supported; adults, labels and cavity habitat remain stable. Exact age, continuous feather sequence, flight ability and fixed sex ratio remain unknown, so scientific verification is false.

证据边界重建：成鸟暗褐眼、黄喙、无耳羽、上胸横斑与下胸纵纹，同基线近等展示，雌鸟仅略丰满；中央年龄未知、尚未攀枝的白绒幼鸟采用短圆绒羽翼芽、隐藏尾部，腹部与双足由不规则腐朽树洞底承托。精确日龄、连续羽序、量化雌雄差异及巢腔几何仍待科学校准；旧v2保留。

## 19-megascops-trichopsis-v5

证据边界重建：三鸟及标签正确；成鸟保留橙调虹膜、黄橄榄色喙、短耳簇、须状脸羽和不规则粗斑，雌鸟仅略丰满；中央年龄未知的较大幼鸟具蓬松中雏羽、短圆发育翼、隐藏尾部和双足枝条支撑。精确日龄、连续羽序、趾羽边界、量化雌雄差异及细斑几何仍待科学校准；旧版本保留。

![19-megascops-trichopsis-v5](concepts/19-megascops-trichopsis-v5.png)


## 20-megascops-clarkii-v3

证据边界重建并修订幼鸟翼部：成鸟红褐云雾林形态、黄眼、灰绿色喙及不过度拉长的下段裸露跗跖通过复查；约三周龄但年龄仍不精确的幼鸟以白绒为主，仅见少量短小桂皮色发育羽尖，无长层叠飞羽或可见尾羽，腹部与双足由天然树洞底承托。连续羽序、裸跗跖边界、分性别测量与普遍巢腔几何仍待科学校准。

![20-megascops-clarkii-v3](concepts/20-megascops-clarkii-v3.png)


## 21-megascops-choliba-v4

证据边界重建并修订幼鸟翼部：同色型成年黄眼、浅绿灰喙、黑缘脸盘、羽跗和近等展示高度已复查；中央11–13日龄幼鸟以白绒为主，仅见少量贴身极短羽轴，无长层叠飞羽、尾羽或耳簇，腹部与双足由近乎裸露的枯棕榈洞底承托。精确羽轴长度、连续羽序、参考亚种、量化雌雄差异及普遍巢腔几何仍待科学校准。

![21-megascops-choliba-v4](concepts/21-megascops-choliba-v4.png)


## 22-gymnasio-nudipes-v3

证据边界重建：同棕色型成年赤褐虹膜、绿黄喙、显著白眉、白底棕纹、自然裸露下腿和近等尺度通过复查；中央年龄未定幼鸟以灰米色蓬松幼羽为主，短圆翼芽藏于绒羽下，无成熟翼尾或成年白眉，腹部和双足由洞底承托。精确日龄、幼鸟虹膜、连续羽序、飞行能力、量化性别差异及精细裸腿几何仍待科学校准。

![22-gymnasio-nudipes-v3](concepts/22-gymnasio-nudipes-v3.png)


## 28-glaucidium-perlatum-v2

证据边界修订：成年黄眼、浅绿黄色喙蜡膜、肉桂褐底整齐白色珠斑、白底棕色纵纹及雌雄近等展示通过复查；成年雌鸟后转展示成对白边黑色假眼。中央年龄未定离巢幼鸟更小、更蓬松，头背珠斑明显减少、短圆折翼、无成熟横斑长尾，双足由低矮金合欢枝承托。精确日龄、逐日羽区与虹膜发育、连续翼尾生长及量化性别差异仍待科学校准。

![28-glaucidium-perlatum-v2](concepts/28-glaucidium-perlatum-v2.png)


## 29-glaucidium-capense-v2

### #76 Striped Owl — *Asio clamator* v2 (2026-10-27)

![#76 Striped Owl v2](concepts/76-asio-clamator-v2.png)

Revision generated and visually reviewed after one juvenile-wing edit. Adults retain brown-cinnamon eyes, black bill/claws, long dark ear tufts, dark-bordered white facial discs, bold vertical streaking and near-equal display height. The approximately five-week brancher has warm yellowish-white/buff body plumage, a defined face and short tufts, a shorter rounded developing wing with diffuse bars, a short tail and two-foot support. A rudimentary grassland ground nest uses straw, leaves and feathers. Exact age, continuous feather sequence, fixed sex ratio and subspecies transfer remain unknown; `scientifically_verified=false`. Sources: `CLA-PONCE-GONZALEZ-2025`, `CLA-OWLPAGES-2020`, `CLA-AOS-2021`, `WHSO-MIKKOLA-2013`. [Run record](../production/runs/2026-10-27T0334+0800-cycle-1.md).


### #77 Stygian Owl — *Asio stygius* v2 (2026-10-27)

![#77 Stygian Owl v2](concepts/77-asio-stygius-v2.png)

Revision generated and visually reviewed after one juvenile-wing edit. A 35-day Ecuador field observation supports an older-than-one-month brancher with yellow eyes, black facial disc, small tufts, pale rear-head feathers, scattered residual down, barred belly, developing herringbone chest lines and branch-changing behavior. The final juvenile has a shorter, softer formed wing rather than adult-length crisp remiges; dark yellow-eyed adults share a common branch baseline with only a modestly fuller female. Exact age, flight threshold, subspecies transfer and sex ratio remain unknown; `scientifically_verified=false`. Sources: `STY-CADENA-2018`, `STY-AMARO-2024`, `STY-SPIZAETUS-2018`, `STY-OWLPAGES-2020`, `WHSO-MIKKOLA-2013`. [Run record](../production/runs/2026-10-27T0334+0800-cycle-2.md).

- [Original PNG](concepts/165-ninox-punctulata-v1.png) · 1536×1024 · 616,088 bytes · SHA-256 `d2ef263bbb2e53cf2c85c9e0606c35d91558d22f5fff4fb8d1fbafa8c928c2d1`

- NEW concept. Three actually-read sources ground the adult identity, Sulawesi stream/forest-edge habitat and mesoptile. First render and indexed archive derivative both passed actual original-resolution review without editing.
- Iteration evidence: the normal “remove adult-like juvenile remiges” safeguard was deliberately *not* applied, because Mikkola explicitly states that this species’ mesoptile wing and tail feathers are as adult. The validated rule is to override a generic juvenile safeguard only with direct species-specific evidence, while retaining juvenile body softness and a fully supported non-flight posture.
- Limits: exact mesoptile age, flight threshold, juvenile iris, nest site and robust sex-size ratio remain unknown; `scientifically_verified=false`.

- #40 *Glaucidium nubicola* v3 — SCIENCE_REVISION; primary record supports a recently fledged juvenile receiving lizard prey, while a tentative secondary caption only weakly supports an unspotted immature crown. [concept](concepts/40-glaucidium-nubicola-v3.png) · [research](../knowledge/species/40-glaucidium-nubicola.md) · [run](../production/runs/20261028T1940+0800-cycle-2.md). ### Incremental evidence-tier rule — tentative immature captions

When a species-specific immature image caption is explicitly tentative (for example “believed to be”), apply only the narrow visible/worded boundary it supports. For a recent fledgling described as unspotted, remove crisp adult spot rows from crown/back/wing while retaining a formed, compact, naturally supported folded wing. Do not infer exact age, downy nestling plumage, flight ability or a full feather sequence. This rule was applied and visually rechecked on #40 v3; broader effectiveness remains unverified.


- #41 *Glaucidium cobanense* v3 — SCIENCE_REVISION; field-research text identifies a brown-morph juvenile and juveniles at the nest entrance, while a 2011 paper documents a brown-morph male at the nest. [concept](concepts/41-glaucidium-cobanense-v3.png) · [research](../knowledge/species/41-glaucidium-cobanense.md) · [run](../production/runs/20261028T2006+0800-cycle-3.md). One juvenile crown/wing edit; exact age, feather chronology, sex metrics and flight remain unknown; science pending.

### Incremental morph-versus-sex rule

A documented rufous/brown morph is not evidence of sexual dichromatism. Unless a source links morph to sex, keep adult male and female in the same morph and encode sex only through sourced features or distinct poses. Applied and visually rechecked on #41 v3; broader effectiveness remains unverified.


- #51 *Strix rufipes* v3 — SCIENCE_REVISION; official Chile MMA evidence corrects the adult pair to a darker male and markedly paler, subtly fuller female. The approximately-one-month warm-buff juvenile remains low and fully supported at a mature-tree cavity rim. [concept](concepts/51-strix-rufipes-v3.png) · [research](../knowledge/species/51-strix-rufipes.md) · [run](../production/runs/20261029T0153+0800-cycle-1.md). One juvenile-wing edit removed adult-like layered remiges; exact feather sequence and quantitative sex ratios remain unknown; science pending.

### Incremental sexual-dichromatism rule

When a species source explicitly documents sex-linked plumage difference, encode that color/value direction separately from size direction. A “female paler” statement does not justify a fixed palette ratio, and a “female larger” direction does not justify dramatic standing-height separation. Applied and visually rechecked on #51 v3 (dark male, paler subtly fuller female at comparable head height); broader effectiveness remains unverified.


- #52 *Strix hylophila* v3 — SCIENCE_REVISION; 32–35-day evidence is rendered as a fully supported nest-leaving brancher, not an independently flying fledgling. [concept](concepts/52-strix-hylophila-v3.png) · [research](../knowledge/species/52-strix-hylophila.md) · [run](../production/runs/20261029T0223+0800-cycle-2.md). One edit removed adult-like juvenile remiges and restored subtle female fullness; science pending.

### Incremental nest-departure rule

A reported nest-leaving age is not automatically evidence of independent flight. Unless locomotor behavior is separately documented, render the young bird fully supported at or beside the nest and describe it as a nest-leaving/brancher stage. Applied and visually rechecked on #52 v3; broader effectiveness remains unverified.


- #54 *Bubo africanus* v3 — SCIENCE_REVISION correcting the approximately-six-week juvenile boundary: well-grown folded wings and visible half-length tail with remaining head/underpart down, while still preflight and fully supported. [concept](concepts/54-bubo-africanus-v3.png) · [research](../knowledge/species/54-bubo-africanus.md) · [run](../production/runs/20261029T0228+0800-cycle-3.md). One juvenile-only edit softened crisp adult-like spot rows but retained evidence-supported wing/tail development. Adults remain same-plumage and near-equal; science pending.

### Incremental stage-specific wing rule — developed does not mean adult-patterned

When a species source explicitly says a preflight juvenile has well-grown wing feathers, do not force an early-down short-wing template. Preserve the sourced development and locomotor boundary separately: well-grown folded wings may coexist with inability to fly, remaining down and a half-length tail. Crisp adult spot rows or fully regular adult remige geometry still require direct evidence. Applied and visually rechecked on #54 v3; broader effectiveness remains unverified.

- #55 *Bubo lacteus* v3 — SCIENCE_REVISION correcting the approximately-60-day nest-leaving boundary: developed juvenile remiges and visible developing tail coexist with remaining down, pink eyelids and inability to fly reliably. [concept](concepts/55-bubo-lacteus-v3.png) · [research](../knowledge/species/55-bubo-lacteus.md) · [run](../production/runs/20261029T0245+0800-cycle-4.md). One bounded edit equalized adult scale and removed crisp adult-like juvenile spots. Science pending.

### Incremental stage-specific rule — locomotor behavior constrains feather depiction

Do not infer a hatchling-like short wing merely because a sourced juvenile cannot yet fly. When species-specific accounts document climbing, hopping or flapping for lift near nest departure, pair that locomotor evidence with developed juvenile remiges while keeping the bird fully supported. Adult-like spot geometry remains excluded without direct evidence. Applied and visually rechecked on #55 v3; broader effectiveness remains unverified.

### Incremental stage-specific rule — age uncertainty is not evidence for uniformly short wings

When a source describes an age-unspecified young bird with adult-like wing/tail structure or barred juvenile feathers, do not collapse it into a generic downy short-wing chick. Preserve the sourced developed structure while marking the age and flight boundary unknown. Use labelled field photographs only for support/pose/environment unless they document locomotor ability. Applied and visually rechecked on #57 v3; broader effectiveness remains unverified.

A species-specific estimate of 8–10 weeks plus observed movement among neighbouring branches and an independent “well-grown fledgling” label is sufficient to reject a tiny down-chick wing-bud depiction, even when first flight is unknown. Render developed folded juvenile wings and supported branch movement; keep flight ability explicitly unknown. Applied and visually rechecked on #58 v3; broader effectiveness remains unverified.

When species-specific evidence puts nest departure around 43–49 days and independent field observations describe nest-leavers as nearly adult-sized, do not render a tiny late nestling with wing buds. Retain developed juvenile wing/tail structure but soften pattern regularity and keep the bird supported and parent-dependent. A dated late-dependent juvenile that is almost adult-sized and nearly finished moulting must not be collapsed into a small white begging chick merely because earlier juvenile accounts describe white barred plumage. ### 2026-10-29 — Flight-capable recent fledglings: keep developed wings, soften age signal instead

- **Observed failure:** an evidence-bounded recent fledgling was initially drawn with correct wing length but crisp, repeated adult-like bars; simultaneously, the adult pair exaggerated a weak sex-size direction.
- **Applied rule:** when field evidence shows young can fly immediately after leaving a hollow, do not regress the design to wing buds or a hidden tail. Preserve developed folded wings, visible tail and gripping support; carry youth through downier head/underparts, broader paler bars, softer edges and sparse streaking. Treat “marginally smaller” adult sex direction as near-equal visual mass unless a source supports a stronger gap.
- **Verified here:** #70 *Ninox strenua* v3 passed a full-image and juvenile-crop recheck after one targeted edit. This rule is applied and verified for this plate only; extension to other taxa requires species-specific evidence.


### 2026-10-29 — Use post-fledging movement records to set capability, not adult plumage

- **Observed failure:** #71 v2 used a recent-fledgling label but retained a short-winged, almost hidden-tail chick silhouette; the v3 first render then overcorrected with adult-like white spot rows.
- **Applied rule:** if a primary study records fledglings roosting several metres high immediately after leaving the hollow, preserve developed folded wings, visible tail and firm grip. Encode youth through species-specific collar, nape, streak density and softer irregular markings rather than reducing flight surfaces or copying adult pattern rows.
- **Verified here:** #71 *Ninox connivens* v3 passed full and crop recheck after one targeted edit. Transfer requires species-specific evidence.

- **Observed failure:** older literature under broad *Ninox scutulata* can describe Japanese populations now separated from the Singapore design taxon; copying its rectrix-ageing details would falsely increase apparent precision.
- **Applied rule:** read and index the source, map the old name, then explicitly exclude population-specific feather/stage claims from direct design transfer unless current taxonomy and geographic applicability are demonstrated.
- **Verified here:** v3 uses Singapore institutional/field evidence for adult identity, habitat and branch-dependent juvenile action; the Japanese rectrix/feeding papers are retained only as excluded leads. The edited plate and crop passed visual recheck; broader transfer still requires species-specific evidence.

### #82 v3 iteration lesson (2026-10-29)

For a 25–28-day African Scops Owl recent fledgling, “developed wing” must not be rendered as adult-regular white spot rows or hard mature remige layering. The prompt and review must independently constrain (1) residual mesoptile/down, (2) soft irregular fresh wing marks, (3) short tail, and (4) both-foot natural support. This rule was applied and visually rechecked here; it is not a substitute for an exact daily feather-tract series.


### #83 v3 iteration lesson (2026-10-29)

For Pallid Scops Owl, a pale bird is not a white-spotted bird: adult scapular marks must remain faint buff-grey within a pale sandy ground, while the diagnostic juvenile uses horizontal barring and woollier contour. Treat adult scapular contrast and juvenile bar direction as separate checks. This rule was applied and visually rechecked once; it does not resolve exact age/subspecies/sex differences.

## 2026-10-29 #86 *Otus elegans* v4 — trait-specific RSD and body/wing decoupling

- Result: SCIENCE_REVISION REVIEWED; fixed archive pending; unique species remains 174/251.
- Research: 2 new / 3 reused / 1 corrected source access. Full primary tables show females average about 12% heavier but natural wing length is effectively equal and males can be slightly longer in head/tarsus; no uniform female-larger rule. Ninety-nine nestlings measured through day 32 support a near-adult body/tarsus around fledging while wing growth remains incomplete.
- Review: the first render made the juvenile too small and adult-spotted; the first edit corrected body scale but restored long mature remiges; the second edit retained near-adult body, shortened/softened the folded wings, removed regular adult white rows and hid the tail. Full 1536×1024 plate and 560×650 crop were reopened. Count/text, adult identity/scale, six-foot support, cavity habitat and selected juvenile boundary pass. Exact day, daily feather tract, tail/iris sequence and first-flight competence remain unknown. Science false.

### Incremental rule — separate dimorphism by trait and late-nestling body from wing growth

Do not translate heavier mean female mass into universally taller adults when wing, tarsus or head measurements differ little or reverse direction. Likewise, when species-specific growth curves show mass/tarsus near adult values before wings finish growing, depict a large-bodied supported late nestling with visibly incomplete soft wings—not a tiny fluff ball and not an adult-wing clone. Applied and visually rechecked on #86 v4; broader effectiveness remains unverified.

## 2026-10-30 #05 Tawny Owl v4 — early post-fledging locomotion boundary

- Original: [05-strix-aluco-v4.jpg](concepts/05-strix-aluco-v4.jpg)
- Research: [species record](../research/species/05-strix-aluco-v4.md) · [source index](../research/SOURCE_INDEX.md)
- Review: one juvenile-wing edit; full 1536×1024 plate and 600×760 juvenile crop reopened.
- Boundary: first-week recent fledgling with a formed folded wing, diffuse juvenile texture, short incomplete tail and complete low support; no confident flight claim.
- Unknown: exact day/sex/subspecies, wing/tail metrics, iris transition, first-flight threshold and universal adult size ratio.
- Scientific status: pending / false; v1 approval retained but not inherited.

### Incremental rule — locomotor immaturity does not require deleting a formed wing

When a species-specific post-fledging study documents poor early locomotion but the selected stage is already outside the nest, correct flight competence through pose, support, concealment and juvenile feather texture. Do not automatically shorten every wing into a nestling stub, and do not retain crisp adult pattern rows. Applied and visually rechecked on #05 v4 after one targeted edit; broader effectiveness remains unverified.

For a documented early-emergence chick, permit short localized sheath nubs supported by known-age imagery, but remove elongated vanes, regular pale spots and adult-like rows. Preserve walking support and do not infer flight from being outside the burrow. Applied and visually rechecked on #09 v4; broader effectiveness remains unverified.

- Original: [10-surnia-ulula-v4.jpg](concepts/10-surnia-ulula-v4.jpg)
- Research: [species record](../research/species/10-surnia-ulula-v4.md) · [source index](../research/SOURCE_INDEX.md)

- Unknown: exact individual day, iris, feather metrics, sex, first-flight timing and regional transfer.
- Scientific status: pending / false; v1 approval retained but not inherited.

### Incremental rule — regionally dated nest departure is not a universal flight date

When an institutional regional account gives a 20–22-day departure before flight and another regional synthesis gives 23–27 days, bind the drawing and label to the selected region. Preserve stage-supported developing wing/tail feathers, but remove adult-regular pattern and length. Applied/rechecked on #10 v4; broader effectiveness unverified.

## 2026-10-30 #22 Puerto Rican Owl v4 — alert short-tuft correction

- Original: [22-gymnasio-nudipes-v4.jpg](concepts/22-gymnasio-nudipes-v4.jpg)
- Research: [species record](../research/species/22-gymnasio-nudipes-v4.md) · [source index](../research/SOURCE_INDEX.md)
- Review: one targeted juvenile-wing edit; full 1536×1024 plate and 520×700 juvenile crop reopened.
- Boundary: relaxed round-headed male, subtly fuller alert female with only very short raised feather points, and age-unknown fluffy cavity nestling with compact down-covered wing bud and complete chest/belly/two-foot support.
- Unknown: exact juvenile age, iris ontogeny, feather tract, first-flight threshold, sexed linear measurements and population transfer.
- Scientific status: pending / false; v1–v3 and historical user-confirmation records preserved.

### Incremental rule — bind short alarm tufts to behavior

When sources describe a normally round head but short feather tufts raised during alarm, render the tufts only on the explicitly alert bird and constrain their length. Do not turn a “no true long ear tufts” statement into an absolute tuftless claim, and do not borrow large horns from related screech-owls. Applied and visually rechecked on #22 v4; broader effectiveness remains species-specific.


## 2026-10-30 #25 Cactus Ferruginous Pygmy-Owl v3 — recent-fledgling correction

- Original: [25-glaucidium-brasilianum-cactorum-v3.jpg](concepts/25-glaucidium-brasilianum-cactorum-v3.jpg)
- Research: [species record](../research/species/25-glaucidium-brasilianum-cactorum-v3.md) · [source index](../research/SOURCE_INDEX.md)
- Review: one targeted juvenile-only edit; full 1536×1024 plate and 520×640 crop reopened.
- Boundary: approximately 20–30-day newly fledged bird in dense thorny cover, plain crown, short soft diffuse wing, much shorter lightly patterned tail, low crouch and complete two-foot branch support.
- Unknown: exact age/sex/iris, remex and rectrix lengths, regional colour and quantitative linear dimorphism.
- Scientific status: pending / false; v1/v2 and fixed subspecies slot preserved.

### Incremental rule — remove early-fledgling adult patterns as a bundle

When a source explicitly says a recent fledgling lacks adult crown/false-eye marks and still has uncoordinated flight, review the crown, wing pattern, remex length and tail together. Fixing only one cue can leave a miniature-adult silhouette. Apply the correction only to the selected stage, preserve full natural support, and do not infer an exact day-specific feather map. Applied and visually rechecked on #25 v3; broader effectiveness remains unverified.


## 2026-10-30 #26 Cuban Pygmy-Owl v4 — full-size juvenal-plumage correction

- Original: [26-glaucidium-siju-v4.jpg](concepts/26-glaucidium-siju-v4.jpg)
- Research: [species record](../research/species/26-glaucidium-siju-v4.md) · [source index](../research/SOURCE_INDEX.md)
- Review: first 1536×1024 render passed without edit; full plate and 520×650 juvenile crop reopened.

- Unknown: exact age, sex, iris transition, flight threshold, feather metrics and subspecies.
- Scientific status: pending / false; v1–v3 and fixed species slot retained.

### Incremental rule — conceal unknown lengths instead of inventing them

When a primary source describes juvenal body plumage but not remex, rectrix or flight chronology, make the supported body cues legible and use down/posture/cavity geometry to conceal unsupported lengths. Do not turn a broad juvenal diagnosis into a precise day-stage. Applied and visually rechecked on #26 v4; broader effectiveness remains unverified.

When a primary field study provides a multi-day entrance window but the daily feather sequence is available only through a secondary citation, bind posture and locomotor claim to the primary window and keep feather/iris claims conservative. Do not convert a secondary exact day into false precision. Applied and visually rechecked on #27 v4; broader effectiveness remains unverified.

## 2026-10-30 #34 Amazonian Pygmy Owl v4 — cavity-young and sex-scale correction

- Original: [34-glaucidium-hardyi-v4.png](concepts/34-glaucidium-hardyi-v4.png)
- Research: [species record](../research/species/34-glaucidium-hardyi-v4.md) · [source index](../research/SOURCE_INDEX.md)

- Boundary: published almost-full-grown young fully supported at an old woodpecker cavity entrance; neutral iris, plain crown, weaker streaking, low-contrast formed wing and no flight claim.
- Adult scale: near equal; Vielliard's single female overlaps measured males and does not establish a general female-larger direction.
- Unknown: exact juvenile age, iris chronology, flight threshold and continuous feather sequence.
- Scientific status: pending / false; v1–v3 and historical user records retained.

### Incremental rule — sparse sex samples do not define display direction

A single sexed specimen at the edge of an overlapping range is not enough to encode population-level reverse size dimorphism. Use near-equal display until species-specific sex-stratified evidence supports direction. Applied and visually rechecked on #34 v4; broader effectiveness remains unverified.

### Evidence rule — juvenile chronology before appearance (applied #42 v4, 2026-10-30)

When a longitudinal source shows that a later nestling age already carries adult-like remiges, rectrices or markings, do not pair that age label with an artificially undeveloped wing. Select an earlier documented stage with compatible posture and support, or leave the age uncertain. For #42 v4 this rule changed the design from an approximately-four-week immature to a supported 16–20 d cavity nestling; two edits removed mature crown/wing ordering, and the final plate/crop passed with limitations. This rule is applied and visually checked here, but it is not a cross-species developmental law.


### 2026-10-30 — adult sex-dimorphism evidence gate applied to #43

When a larger live-bird morphometric sample finds no statistically significant sex difference, do not preserve a visible female-size or sex-specific plumage cue from an older small-sample inference. Non-significant trends such as the Forest Owlet female talon mean (p=.071) remain research notes, not drawing rules. This rule was actually applied and visually rechecked on #43 v4: male/female apparent bulk was equalized in edit 2. Validation is limited to this plate; broader back-review is pending.


Verification 2026-10-30T16:45:00+08:00: the equal-size adult rule was effective on #43 v4 after fixed-commit readback; this validates application on this plate only, not yet a whole-archive back-review.


### 2026-10-30 — nestling feather-growth timing gate applied to #44

If a known-age study shows feathers emerging early and wing/sheath length tracking age, do not depict a late nestling as pin-tip-only. Show age-consistent growing vane sections while still withholding adult-length remiges. Adult sex-size evidence must not be transferred to an unsexed chick before the documented onset. Applied and visually rechecked on #44 v4; broader validation pending.


Verification 2026-10-30T16:56:00+08:00: #44 v4 confirms the wing-growth timing gate on this plate; whole-archive validation remains pending.

### 2026-10-31 — dependent-juvenile support gate, #75 application

- Discovery: field evidence for an active branch-dependent juvenile can support developed dark folded wing and short tail without supporting an exact age or full tract chronology.
- Applied: #75 *Ninox scutulata* v4 kept that documented stage but required both gripping feet to read clearly on the branch; the first render failed that support gate and received one targeted edit.
- Verified locally: edited master, crop and derivative show bilateral grip while retaining the evidence-bounded wing/tail stage. This is an instance result, not a universal owl-development rule.


### 2026-10-31 — stage-comparison layout must not invent biology, #76 application

- Discovery: a plate can move the evidence-bounded juvenile to the central comparison slot and align adult support baselines without changing age, sex or morphology claims.
- Applied: #76 *Asio clamator* v4 restored the exact repository number, centered the already supported 25–30-day warm-brown second-stage chick, and kept adults near-equal because the field paper did not establish size direction.
- Verified locally: full master, juvenile crop and derivative preserve bilateral support and immature wing/tail; first render passed without edit. This validates the layout procedure only, not a universal sex-dimorphism rule.


Remote verification 2026-10-31T01:21:30+08:00: #76 v4 fixed archive `75672034f71146d4201c91e41ad710c1c987ed14` returned exact assets in a non-truncated 2,159-entry tree; manifests 580/579/570 matched. The layout procedure is verified for this instance; science remains false.


### 2026-10-31 — dated flight incapacity must control wing geometry, #77 application

- Discovery: when a primary nest study states that poorly developed remiges and rectrices prevented flight at a dated stage, a compact body alone must not be paired with a long layered wing panel.
- Applied: #77 *Asio stygius* v4 retained the large 26–27-day torso but reduced both wings to down-dominated stubs with only short dark tips and an extremely short tail.
- Verified locally: one targeted edit passed full master, crop and derivative review with bilateral ground support. This is validated for the dated Brazilian nest stage, not transferred automatically to later branch-capable juveniles.


Remote verification 2026-10-31T01:39:53+08:00: #77 v4 fixed archive `d3c0236ead2d984344b9ec906cf430a47171e649` returned exact assets in a non-truncated 2,165-entry tree; manifests 581/580/571 matched. The poor-remige geometry correction is verified for this instance; science remains false.


### 2026-10-31 — population fledging estimate must not become an exact individual label, #83 application

- Discovery: “fledging at about 26 days” in a small population study does not date an individual generated bird from appearance; female-heavier mass also does not provide a visible crown-height ratio.
- Applied: #83 *Otus brucei* v4 uses RECENT FLEDGLING, a compact soft juvenile wing/short tail, bilateral cavity-lip support and near-equal adult crown height.
- Verified locally: the first v4 render retained an adult-like repeated pale wing row; one targeted edit removed that leakage. Full master, crop and derivative passed. This validates the prompt/check rule for this instance; science remains false.

### 2026-10-31 — #122 *Otus megalotis* v3 pixel-evidence reconciliation

- A prior production note claimed the juvenile wing/tail had already been shortened, but opening the archived v2 at original resolution showed a long crisp adult-like wing panel and visible long tail. Text records are therefore not accepted as proof of a visual correction; each latest image must be reopened before reuse.
- Fresh evidence supports an age-unknown mobile brancher with formed flight feathers and natural climbing/gripping support, but not an adult-performance pose or literal measured wing geometry. V3 keeps a compact softened remige cluster instead of deleting all flight feathers.
- The initial v3 render again leaked repeated pale adult covert rows. One juvenile-only edit removed those rows, shortened/softened the wing and concealed the tail. Full master, juvenile crop and feet/support crop were reopened and passed with limitations.
- Applied rule: correct maturity through wing length, edge softness and pattern regularity while preserving any species-specific evidence for formed remiges. This instance is visually verified; broader cross-species effectiveness remains unverified. Scientific status remains false.
- [Concept](concepts/122-otus-megalotis-v3.png) · [research](../research/species/122-otus-megalotis-v3.md) · [run](../production/runs/20261031T150240+0800-cycle-1.md)
